It has financial benefits , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; Co-housing gives people of all ages a chance to be part of a community and contribute . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Concorde members believe it will take at least 25 people to get the project off the ground , just because so many people are unable to commit . They also know that they face a red-hot market for raw land . The group had considered buying a small apartment building and renovating , but these kinds of buildings rarely come up for sale , and when they do they&amp;rsquo ; re snapped up quickly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s a Catch-22 . People don&amp;rsquo ; t want to commit until they know where it would be built , &amp;rdquo ; said Thacker Smith . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;7085;TOOLONG ... title= " Members of Concorde , front from left : Valerie Thacker Smith , Margery Street , Mary Huang , Diana Armour , Jane Keeler , and back row from left : Caroline Balderston Parry , Elliot Sherman , Jennifer Craven , and Jake Morrison of Concorde cohousing Saturday November 30 , 2019 . Ashley Fraser/Postmedia " data-portal-copyright= " Ashley Fraser " **28;7121;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1516041 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Concorde member Margery Street has visited cohousing in B.C. , where the Canadian Cohousing Network lists nine communities as completed and another four as under construction . Part of the reason for the success on the west coast is that there&amp;rsquo ; s a financial institution willing to advance money until projects are completed , said Street . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; No one has really stepped up in Ontario , &amp;rdquo ; said Huang. &amp;ldquo ; You need a bank or financial institution that understands the concept . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Legally , cohousing takes a lot of attention to detail , especially the what-ifs as members age . What if a member develops dementia ? What if someone remarries and the new spouse doesn&amp;rsquo ; t subscribe to the philosophy ? What about adult children who return to the nest ? Some communities have legal wording that gives the surviving members the first right of refusal if a unit is sold so the philosophy can remain intact . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; MacDiarmid sees herself losing freedom as she ages . Already she doesn&amp;rsquo ; t drive at night . The members of Tay Commons don&amp;rsquo ; t plan to be personal support workers for each other , but they would like to share resources , such as driving for groceries . They have pledged to remain a community , even without a common roof over their heads . Some already live within walking distance of each other , and others may join them as houses in the neighbourhood come up for sale . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; How do you maintain the philosophy of cohousing without a house ? The challenge for aging seniors is how to develop community without an actual building , &amp;rdquo ; said Burt . &amp;ldquo ; There is a desire , even if we don&amp;rsquo ; t have a house , to be as close as possible . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;7151;TOOLONG ... title= " Steven Fick in front of his home in Terra Frima . He bought into the cohousing community in 1997 and still lives there . " alt= " Steven Fick in front of his home in Terra Frima . He bought into the cohousing community in 1997 and still lives there . " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;7187;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1517262 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The original members of Terra Firma are getting older . But so far , none have left , said Fick. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We will deal with that when it happens . People want to age here . That might mean needing help with care or meals . It&amp;rsquo ; s a creative process , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; I think part of the attraction is that Terra Firma is like an extended family . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;7217;TOOLONG ... title= " Steven Fick and his neighbour Suzanne talk in their back yard in Ottawa Tuesday Dec 3 , 2019 . Steven is part of Terra Firma , a cohousing community on Drummond Street in Ottawa . " alt= " Steven Fick and his neighbour Suzanne talk in their back yard in Ottawa Tuesday Dec 3 , 2019 . Steven is part of Terra Firma , a cohousing community on Drummond Street in Ottawa . " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;7253;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1518295 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Other intentional communities may have a shared religion , a charismatic leader or a utopian philosophy , said Fick. &amp;ldquo ; Cohousing is more down-to-earth and practical . It&amp;rsquo ; s just people trying to figure out how to live closer . For me , it&amp;rsquo ; s not about utopia . It&amp;rsquo ; s about making it as good as it can be under the circumstances . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The members of Convivium are regrouping and plan to have a refined vision within the next few months , said Markell. &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re guinea pigs and we&amp;rsquo ; re choosing it . With some luck , I think we&amp;rsquo ; ll be able to show what&amp;rsquo ; s possible . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Concorde is still looking for more members &amp;mdash ; and land . &amp;ldquo ; We need more members to help do the work since it&amp;rsquo ; s not a simple and easy process , &amp;rdquo ; said Huang. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I know how much richer my life is , &amp;rdquo ; said Fick of his life in Terra Firma. &amp;ldquo ; I have a life that is worth living and I see my life as significant to other people . A lot of other people have my back . That gives me a lot of inner peace . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; **28;7283;TOOLONG THE FEATURE&lt;/strong&gt; : &lt;a href= " https : **33;7313;TOOLONG ... cost of loneliness : Canadians are facing a solitary future -- and it 's affecting their **27;7348;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;This article was written with the support of a journalism fellowship from The Gerontological Society of America , Journalists Network on Generations and the Silver Century Foundation . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Joanne LauciusThu , 19 Dec 2019 21:11:03 **45;7377;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal NewsSeniorsThe cost of loneliness : Canadians are facing a solitary future -- and it 's affecting their healthhttp : **34;7424;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;7460;TOOLONG ... title= " Ken Roberts , 56 , lives alone and has felt lonely and depressed . But he realizes that his health depends on his ability to connect with other people . " alt= " Ken Roberts , 56 , lives alone and has felt lonely and depressed . But he realizes that his health depends on his ability to connect with other people . " data-portal-copyright= " JULIE OLIVER " **28;7496;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1526268 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;7526;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ken Roberts&amp;rsquo ; life in the past few years has been the perfect storm of loneliness and social isolation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Roberts was a caregiver to his ailing father for six years , a stressed-out witness to a string of hospitalizations and descent into dementia . The two shared an apartment and were alone most of the time . Roberts&amp;rsquo ; father died in 2015 , followed by his brother in 2017 . Roberts has had depression and was diagnosed with Type 2 diabetes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I have pretty much always been introverted , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; I was used to being in the apartment all the time . It became almost a habit . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Roberts , 56 , also realizes that his health depends on his ability to make a point of getting out of his apartment and doing things with other people . He joined a cooking club and a walking group . On a recent Thursday , Roberts could be found rocking out with the &amp;ldquo ; talent optional music jam&amp;rdquo ; at the Centretown Community Health Centre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If you&amp;rsquo ; re interacting with a group , people are expecting you . You&amp;rsquo ; re motivated , &amp;rdquo ; said Roberts . &amp;ldquo ; When you&amp;rsquo ; re by yourself , it&amp;rsquo ; s easy to get into that negativity spiral . It&amp;rsquo ; s important to get that reinforcement from people . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;7562;TOOLONG ... title= " Health issues like diabetes and depression and being a caregiver to his ailing father , Ken Roberts , 56 , found himself not only alone , but lonely , which can have detrimental health effects . " alt= " Health issues like diabetes and depression and being a caregiver to his ailing father , Ken Roberts , 56 , found himself not only alone , but lonely , which can have detrimental health effects . " data-portal-copyright= " JULIE OLIVER " **28;7598;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1518197 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the space of a few short years , loneliness has gone from a subject for poets and songwriters to a public health conundrum to be solved &amp;mdash ; not just to promote individual happiness , but as a potential money-saver for the health-care system . Some studies have linked chronic loneliness to the risk of early death , coronary heart disease , stroke , cognitive decline and dementia . People who are lonely are more prone to depression . Loneliness and a lack of social interaction are predictive of suicide among older people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2015 , researchers at the University of Manitoba&amp;rsquo ; s Centre on Aging reported that among people 45-plus who were hospitalized , being lonely was associated with a higher likelihood of returning to hospital . Being more socially isolated was associated with a higher likelihood of remaining in hospital for longer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; All this evidence is piling up at the same time that shifting demographics point to a solitary future for many Canadians . The 2016 census found that , for the first time in recorded Canadian history , one-person households are the most common household type in the country . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As time passes , the reasons for living alone are also changing . In 2016 , about 22 per cent of those living alone were widowed , down from 33 per cent in 1981 . But 32 per cent of those living alone were separated or divorced , nearly triple the rate in 1981. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The whole issue of aging alone is a huge issue for Canada for a number of reasons , &amp;rdquo ; said Nora Spinks , CEO of the Vanier Institute of the Family . &amp;ldquo ; You have densely-populated urban environments , where you can be alone in a crowd . There are suburban environments where lack of transportation can lead to social isolation . And then there are the distances for people in rural areas and the north . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Former U.S. surgeon general Vivek Murthy has called loneliness a &amp;ldquo ; growing health epidemic . &amp;rdquo ; It has been estimated that socially isolated seniors in the U.S. face a 29 per cent greater risk of death &amp;mdash ; and they cost $134 more a month in Medicare spending . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Being disconnected is just as dangerous to good health as smoking 15 cigarettes a day , and is more predictive of early death than the effects of air pollution or being a couch potato , according to oft-cited research by Julianne Holt-Lunstad of Brigham Young University . Another study of 5,100 Americans and their social contacts published in 2008 suggested that loneliness is contagious . When one person feels lonely , it can spread to others in the network . Over time , every additional day of loneliness per week led to about an extra day of loneliness per month for those in the social network . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; All of this has called for a response from policy-makers . The United Kingdom has a minister of loneliness , a national strategy , a Campaign to End Loneliness and The Silver Line , a free and confidential call centre for people 55 and over . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Australia has the Coalition to End Loneliness . The U.S. has the doubled-barrelled Coalition to End Social Isolation and Loneliness . This year , the Dignity in Aging Act in the U.S. , which updates the 1965 Older Americans Act , put a major focus on social isolation and empowering local organizations to evaluate solutions for social isolation , incorporating social isolation screening into health and supportive services for seniors and creating a new focus on the issue of social isolation among older adults at the national level . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There is no other health condition that affects half of Americans , &amp;rdquo ; Andrew MacPherson , the director of the U.S. Coalition to End Social Isolation and Loneliness , told attendees at The Gerontological Society of America&amp;rsquo ; s annual scientific meeting last month . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Nobody saw this issue a year and a half ago , and we&amp;rsquo ; re already getting legislation in Washington . This is starting to get traction . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;7628;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;7670;TOOLONG ... is a potentially key way to combat loneliness -- but it 's difficult to get off the **27;7705;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Businesses are also responding to the issues of loneliness . One example is a &amp;ldquo ; concierge&amp;rdquo ; program that arranges rides from the ride-sharing service Lyft &lt;span class= " ILfuVd NA6bn " &gt;&lt;span class= " e24Kjd " &gt;for home-care clients who do n't have a smartphone or prefer to arrange their transportation by phone . **26;7734;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " ILfuVd NA6bn " &gt;&lt;span class= " e24Kjd " &gt; " **26;7762;TOOLONG The market is starting to react to the idea that loneliness is a condition that can be managed , &amp;rdquo ; said MacPherson. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While Canada doesn&amp;rsquo ; t have a national strategy , the issue is on the radar . The International Federation of Ageing report commissioned by Employment and Social Development Canada concluded that the top emerging issue facing seniors is keeping them socially connected and active . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last fall during the municipal election campaign , Ottawa Mayor Jim Watson promised an extra day of free transit for seniors . &amp;ldquo ; This measure is not only a cost-saving opportunity for seniors , it encourages them to leave their home and take part in social outings , helping to fight widespread loneliness and isolation affecting many seniors , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Everyone is looking for an answer about how to fix this , &amp;rdquo ; said Mary Pat Sullivan , a professor of social work and social gerontology at Nipissing University who recently returned to Canada from the U.K. , where she researched social isolation and loneliness for more than 20 years . &amp;ldquo ; I can see that interest is starting to pick up here . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The first &amp;ldquo ; summit on social isolation and companion animals&amp;rdquo ; was held in Washington last May , attracting public-health officials , gerontologists and veterinary researchers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Just last month , New York Times columnist Nicholas Kristof urged a war on loneliness and suggested it should be easier for people to have dogs because research has shown that dog owners&amp;nbsp ; &amp;mdash ; but not cat owners &amp;mdash ; are less lonely . ( Not exactly true . The growing body of research is mixed on whether pet ownership is a sure-fire balm for loneliness. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In October , menshealth.com posted an article arguing that men form friendships differently than women and need different ways to ward off loneliness . The headline ? &amp;ldquo ; Loneliness is fatal . Video games can keep men alive . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Free bus tickets , dogs and video games are only the start of the responses . Manitoba observes Let No One Be Alone Week , which encourages people to reach out to seniors . Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s Hey , Neighbour ! was a pilot project aimed at connecting apartment-dwellers through activities ranging from board game nights to an emergency preparedness workshop . The connections were organized by &amp;ldquo ; resident animators. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;7790;TOOLONG ... title= " R ? mi-Serge Gratton , front centre , and Ken Roberts , front right , belt it out at the " talent optional music jam " at the Centretown Community Health Centre . " alt= " R ? mi-Serge Gratton , front centre , and Ken Roberts , front right , belt it out at the " talent optional music jam " at the Centretown Community Health Centre . " data-portal-copyright= " JULIE OLIVER " **28;7826;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1516755 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Ontario , 11 community health centres , including the Centretown Community Health Centre , were part of a pilot project to issue &amp;ldquo ; social prescriptions&amp;rdquo ; for people who feel disconnected . The prescriptions were for activities ranging from dance lessons to museum visits to boost social connections . Doctors , nurses and other health professionals who identify social disconnectedness can refer clients to &amp;ldquo ; link workers&amp;rdquo ; who find social opportunities tailored to the client . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The project was based on similar strategies in the U.K. , where the National Health Service is fired up enough about social prescribing that it calls it a &amp;ldquo ; key component&amp;rdquo ; of universal personalized care . About 59 per cent of general practitioners think social prescribing can help reduce their workload . Last January , the NHS announced it was hiring 1,000 more link workers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ontario pilot project is wrapping up this month and a final report is due out in March , but a progress report noted that for some clients , spending time with others and socializing was a motivation for leaving home . One Ottawa client reported that a kinesiologist at the centre had suggested walking three times a week , but she found the motivation was greater after she joined a walking group . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We do better with others , &amp;rdquo ; said Natasha Beaudin , the Centretown Community Health Centre&amp;rsquo ; s project lead . Social prescribing has also helped to keep staff attuned to checking on loneliness in their interactions with clients . &amp;ldquo ; Living in downtown Ottawa can be pretty isolating , &amp;rdquo ; said Beaudin. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ken Roberts said activities like the talent optional music jam are a reason to leave his apartment . &amp;ldquo ; Unless I motivate myself , I just go to fortress mentality . I have to come up with strategies to motivate myself and interact with the world , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; R&amp;eacute ; mi-Serge Gratton , another member of the music jam , said it&amp;rsquo ; s important to feel like he&amp;rsquo ; s part of a community . Social prescribing is another way for people who are lonely to have an incentive to get out , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; When you&amp;rsquo ; re negative or depressed , your body has no resistance to anything else . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;7856;TOOLONG ... title= " The Centretown Community Health Centre has been part of a pilot project on " social prescribing " in which people who are identified as isolated are " prescribed " social activities , which can better overall well being and lower the health costs of isolation and loneliness . " alt= " The Centretown Community Health Centre has been part of a pilot project on " social prescribing " in which people who are identified as isolated are " prescribed " social activities , which can better overall well being and lower the health costs of isolation and loneliness . " data-portal-copyright= " JULIE OLIVER " **28;7892;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1518194 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Loneliness and social isolation might sound similar , but they are two different things . Social isolation is about the number and frequency of links in a person&amp;rsquo ; s social network . It is objective . Loneliness is a subjective thing &amp;mdash ; it&amp;rsquo ; s an individual reaction to the quality of those connections . It is possible to be socially isolated without being lonely . Two people may have completely different perceptions of the same social network . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You can have two people with the same network , and one person will be satisfied and the other will not , &amp;rdquo ; said Nancy Newall , an associate professor of psychology at Brandon University and loneliness researcher . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It has become increasingly clear that loneliness is a nuanced issue . The relationship between loneliness and health is a chicken-and-egg question . Loneliness can make people sick &amp;mdash ; people who are alone are less likely to take necessary medication , exercise or eat properly . Being sick can also contribute to loneliness . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Newall and fellow researcher Verena Menec have argued that the concepts of social isolation and loneliness should be considered together . Those who are &amp;ldquo ; lonely in a crowd&amp;rdquo ; may have a different health trajectory and benefit from different interventions than someone who is isolated and lonely , they concluded . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last June , the Angus Reid Institute released a study of social isolation in Canada , probing the number and frequency of social connections as well as the relative satisfaction with the quality of those connections . The study divided the respondents into five categories : the desolate ; lonely but not isolated ; isolated but not lonely ; moderately connected and cherished . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among the key findings : the cherished group &amp;mdash ; those who suffered from neither social isolation nor loneliness &amp;mdash ; are most likely to be married with children and higher incomes . Those 55-plus with incomes of less than $50,000 were twice as likely to be in the &amp;ldquo ; desolate&amp;rdquo ; group &amp;mdash ; they were both lonely and isolated , and were more than twice as likely as the cherished to be single and living alone . Almost a quarter of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s population fits into the desolate group , according to Angus Reid . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the U.K. , the focus on disconnectedness and its health effects went from being a grassroots movement to a national system in three years , said Sonia Hsiung , the lead on Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s social prescribing pilot projects . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Which is really speedy . I wonder if it&amp;rsquo ; s because everyone relates to it . Everyone likely has personal experience with social isolation , or they know someone who has . Social isolation resonates with everyone . I think it has captured the public&amp;rsquo ; s imagination . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In terms of a policy response to social isolation and loneliness , Hsiung doesn&amp;rsquo ; t think the Canadian health-care system is there yet &amp;mdash ; although she notes that the Ontario Health Teams , announced earlier this year by the province as a way to integrate and co-ordinate health care among agencies , are a good way to incorporate screening for loneliness and social isolation into heath care . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The momentum is growing , the need is growing , &amp;rdquo ; said Hsiung. &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re at the stage of planning how to build this into the health-care system . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;7922;TOOLONG ... title= " A physical health class for seniors has been part of the Centretown Community Health Centre 's repertoire for over a decade and , while it was n't intended as part of it 's recent initiative of " social prescribing , " such classes that bring people together have many great unintended consequences . " alt= " A physical health class for seniors has been part of the Centretown Community Health Centre 's repertoire for over a decade and , while it was n't intended as part of it 's recent initiative of " social prescribing , " such classes that bring people together have many great unintended consequences . " data-portal-copyright= " JULIE OLIVER " **28;7958;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1518195 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Angus Reid poll pointed out that it is possible to be lonely but not isolated . About 10 per cent of Canadians fit into this group . About four in 10 of these are under the age of 35 , and they score the highest in terms of having a university education . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another 15 per cent of Canadians felt isolated , but not lonely . Almost half of this group is 55 or older . Six out of 10 are married and almost half have children over 18 . Many are empty nesters. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The poll also found that 35 per cent of the &amp;ldquo ; lonely but not isolated&amp;rdquo ; group wished family and friends would spend more time visiting and socializing . Only two per cent of those who were &amp;ldquo ; isolated but not lonely&amp;rdquo ; said the same thing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The young are also more lonely than the elderly , surveys have repeatedly discovered . The BBC&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; loneliness experiment , &amp;rdquo ; an online survey that attracted 55,000 responses from around the world , found that 40 per cent of 16 to 24-year-old respondents said they often or very often feel lonely , compared with 27 per cent of those over 75 . Young people who took the survey reported higher levels of loneliness , no matter what their gender or country . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Should governments be in the business of alleviating loneliness ? There has been a bit of a &amp;ldquo ; moral panic&amp;rdquo ; around loneliness , said Christina Victor , a professor of gerontology and public health at Brunel University London . She feels that the coverage about loneliness in the U.K. has been alarmist , stigmatized the lonely and painted a picture of miserable older people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is also a lack of consensus about what should be done about loneliness &amp;mdash ; and some surprising findings about the experience of it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; About four-fifths of the respondents in the Angus Reid survey said they like spending time on their own . Only a third believe loneliness is about being on your own . People who said they were always alone were more likely to say they felt lonely . But those who live alone are only slightly more likely to feel lonely more often , the survey found . The finding suggests that living alone isn&amp;rsquo ; t as much of a problem as spending lots of time alone . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Victor&amp;nbsp ; is also skeptical about claims of adverse health outcomes linked to loneliness . A lot of studies that connect loneliness with poor health outcomes don&amp;rsquo ; t take into account other factors , such as widowhood . Disability and major life events , such as the loss of a spouse , can put older adults at risk of social isolation or loneliness . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Research has been flawed in terms of trying to untangle the causes and consequences , said Sullivan , the social work professor . &amp;ldquo ; Is loneliness worse than 15 cigarettes ? Why not 14 cigarettes ? It&amp;rsquo ; s an appealing headline , but we need more evidence . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although there have been links in the scientific literature between loneliness and conditions such as dementia , there is less evidence in the scientific literature on the effectiveness of interventions aimed at reducing loneliness , said Newall , the psychology professor . There is also a dispute over using terms like &amp;ldquo ; epidemic . &amp;rdquo ; It suggests loneliness is a contagious medical condition that can be contained . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Can loneliness be a good thing ? About 41 per cent of the respondents in the BBC project said there were positives . Nevertheless , they wanted to experience it a lot less . About 80 per cent had experienced loneliness at other points in their lives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The experience may be very fleeting . It could last for a moment , a day or longer than that , &amp;rdquo ; said Sullivan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Loneliness is not always bad , &amp;rdquo ; said Victor , who argues that there&amp;rsquo ; s a need for skepticism when it comes to claims that loneliness causes health conditions such as cognitive decline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;7988;TOOLONG ... title= " Loneliness is an issue around the world . " alt= " Loneliness is an issue around the world . " data-portal-copyright= " KatarzynaBialasiewicz " **28;8024;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1518229 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada has framed the discussion as &amp;ldquo ; social isolation&amp;rdquo ; rather than &amp;ldquo ; loneliness , &amp;rdquo ; said Sullivan . She believes this is a good thing . Loneliness is an experience that depends on the individual . Social isolation is a more neutral term , and from a research perspective , it is more quantifiable , she said . Framing it as social isolation normalizes the condition , and makes it a matter of social inclusion rather than turning loneliness into an &amp;ldquo ; older people&amp;rdquo ; problem . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sullivan doesn&amp;rsquo ; t think there&amp;rsquo ; s a need to appoint a minister of loneliness in Canada . &amp;ldquo ; They&amp;rsquo ; ve gone to the extreme in the U.K. But I see it shifting . They&amp;rsquo ; ve drawn attention to the fact that it&amp;rsquo ; s not a problem of old age . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Loneliness is a normal part of being human , said Newall. &amp;ldquo ; What we need to be concerned about is when it becomes chronic . It starts to really impact the way you see the world . If people experience failures in making and keeping friends , they start to withdraw , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s an emotion that all humans have . Loneliness is important , like all emotions . It tells us that something isn&amp;rsquo ; t right . That makes us aware that maybe we can change things . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sullivan agrees . She recalls a recent conversation with an Indigenous scholar who told her that &amp;ldquo ; loneliness is a teacher . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I stopped for a moment . And I thought : &amp;lsquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s teaching us that we need to get out , to reach out to friends or family , ' &amp;rdquo ; said Sullivan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We may not be able to get rid of it because we&amp;rsquo ; re humans . We may not want to get rid of it because it may be a necessary experience . It may be the cue to an individual that they need to engage . Maybe we have to learn to be alone . Learn how to talk about it when we feel lonely , to minimize the pain . Or to embrace it sometimes . Introspection may be an opportunity . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Roberts still considers himself to be a work in progress . He has friends he feels are as close as family . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m at the point of my life where I have to start implementing changes or I face rapid decline , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; I have to resist the urge to think things aren&amp;rsquo ; t going to get better . It&amp;rsquo ; s easy to fall into morose , self-pitying despair . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;This article was written with the support of a journalism fellowship from The Gerontological Society of America , Journalists Network on Generations and the Silver Century Foundation . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;8054;TOOLONG ... title= " Ken Roberts . " alt= " Ken Roberts . " data-portal-copyright= " JULIE OLIVER " **28;8090;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1518198 " /&gt; Joanne LauciusThu , 19 Dec 2019 13:13:51 **45;8120;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLife StoryNationalSeniors'We can do better ' : City 's first health team to include community health centreshttp : **33;8167;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;8202;TOOLONG ... title= " Ontario Health Minister Christine Elliott " alt= " Ontario Health Minister Christine Elliott " data-portal-copyright= " Tijana Martin/The Canadian Press " **28;8238;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1365530 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; A new model of health care delivery is set to begin in Ottawa with the announcement of the city&amp;rsquo ; s first health team . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ottawa Health Team &amp;mdash ; one of the groups designed to implement organizing and deliver health care in a new way &amp;mdash ; will get the green light from Health Minister Christine Elliott on Friday , this newspaper has learned . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Elliott , Ontario Minister of Health and Long Term Care , has begun announcing the first of what will initially be two dozen such health teams across the province in recent days . She was scheduled to make an announcement in Ottawa Friday morning . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eventually , the province plans to organize all health care into such teams , including hospitals , doctors , home care , clinics and other organizations . The teams are tasked with organizing patient care and smoothing transition for patients from different care providers and services . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The government originally said there could be up to 50 such teams around Ontario . But after receiving 150 applications from potential teams , Elliott conceded that there might eventually be more . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For now , 24 teams will be launched around the province as early adopters of the new system , including the Ottawa Health Team , which will include the region&amp;rsquo ; s major hospital , The Ottawa Hospital , as well as seven community health centres , Ottawa Public Health , Inner City Health , Bruy&amp;egrave ; re , and others . Among its aims are providing patient care and support in the most appropriate settings &amp;ldquo ; to achieve better outcomes and reduce costs . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The teams represent a key pillar of the Ford government&amp;rsquo ; s health restructuring agenda . But members of the public might not notice a huge change at first &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of the keys to the teams is that they will co-ordinate patient care . That means , for example , that patient information will be shared among partners in the team and patients will have help navigating the system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Elliott and others have used the example of easing patients&amp;rsquo ; transition from hospital to home care &amp;mdash ; something that can be disjointed and stressful under the current system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Through an Ontario Health Team , patients will experience easier transitions from one provider to another , including , for example , between hospitals and home care providers , with one patient story , one patient record and one care plan , &amp;rdquo ; Elliott said this week . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That co-ordination could take some time to be complete , though . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although the province has begun approving health teams under the new system , home care agencies still exist under the old system and are coordinated by ( local health integration networks ( known as LHINs ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ford government has announced that it plans to get rid of the LHINs &amp;mdash ; the system of 14 regional health coordinating bodies set up by the previous Liberal government &amp;mdash ; but that hasn&amp;rsquo ; t happened yet . So , for now , LHINs will continue to be in charge of home care . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eventually , home and community care functions will transfer to Ontario health teams , said Hayley Chazan , spokesperson for Elliott . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The ministry is planning carefully for these future changes and will have more information about timelines and funding models at a later date , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Officials with the Ottawa Health Team say they believe the various organizations working together can do a better job of keeping people healthy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We know that when people get sick and need health care , the current system is comprised of many silos , making it difficult for people to access care and navigate the transitions along the continuum of care , &amp;rdquo ; the Ottawa Health Team wrote in its submission to the province . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; By working together as a team , we believe we can do better at keeping people well and offering more seamless care when they need it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The health team is being led by the city&amp;rsquo ; s community health centres . Many health teams are led by hospitals , which are the biggest players . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ottawa Health Team was one of three local teams invited to submit full applications to become some of the first teams in the province . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; CHEO submitted an application to become a child-centred Kids First health team with an early focus on children with complex care and mental health needs and Montfort Hospital is part of a group that submitted an application to become a francophone focused health team . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is unclear whether more than one Ottawa team will be among the group of early approvals. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; epayne@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;8268;TOOLONG ...
