" data-portal-copyright= " Julie Oliver " **28;16731;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1406167 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Anxiety levels are high among some physicians at The Ottawa Hospital as it transitions to a new electronic health record system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One doctor , who contacted this newspaper , said some physicians feel they were not adequately trained before the June 1 launch and are not receiving the support they need . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We feel abandoned . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a statement , the hospital&amp;rsquo ; s director of communications and engagement , Kate Eggins , said that 98 per cent of physicians had training . Additional training and support were also provided . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We take concerns of staff and physicians seriously and we encourage them to speak openly to colleagues and management when they have a concern . we have always acknowledged that moving from a paper system to a digital one would require substantial change to workflows , processes and procedures . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The doctor also said the number of glitches in the system is much higher than physicians had expected and will take months , not weeks , to correct . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I am shocked by the amount of glitches . The system is in relative chaos right now . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ottawa Hospital is one of six Eastern Ontario health organizations to adopt the Epic electronic health record system . Several other Ontario hospitals , including CHEO , had previously adopted the U.S.-developed system that is used around the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. Alan Karovitch , an internal medicine doctor who is vice-president of the medical staff association at The Ottawa Hospital , agreed that the transition has been stressful. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; People have to understand this is the biggest process change any of us will see in our careers . Change management is hard . But if you keep your eye on the prize and contextualize it , you realize this is OK. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;16761;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;16803;TOOLONG ... woman 's appendix bursts during lengthy wait to be admitted to **29;16838;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;16869;TOOLONG ... record system brings some delays , frustrations at The Ottawa **29;16904;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Karovitch said he has been well supported throughout the process and , although there have been frustrating days , he has always felt supported , confident about patient care and that things are improving . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think it is good for patient care &amp;mdash ; no more sticky notes on charts , no more faxes . We are getting out of that . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The doctor who spoke to this newspaper and a second physician said that they are concerned about potential errors and delays during the glitch-prone rollout of the Epic system , as well as expanding wait lists for appointments and non-urgent surgery . The doctor said he had issued eight &amp;ldquo ; tickets&amp;rdquo ; to report issues with the system and has not even received an acknowledgment about when they will be fixed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This newspaper has been told that 10,000 tickets &amp;mdash ; representing issues with the system &amp;mdash ; were filed within the first two days after its launch . He agreed to speak on the condition of anonymity , saying he feared for his job . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The transition is adding significantly to physicians&amp;rsquo ; workloads , the doctor said , tripling the amount of work being done in some cases . That means they are spending more time looking at computers than with patients and is slowing things down . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In some cases it has led to delays in transferring patients from one campus to another . It is also increasing the risk of errors in scheduling tests and medication . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The irony is that so many things don&amp;rsquo ; t work , we have the worst of both worlds . We still have to fax things , &amp;rdquo ; said the doctor . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another physician said the new system has resulted in chaos and low morale and set the stage for dangerous situations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Duplicate bookings , the physician said , are common . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A woman who was taken to the General campus by ambulance on June 2 , the day after Epic launched , experienced that . She said she was on her way to having a second , unnecessary , CT scan before she questioned why and health workers discovered it was an error . This newspaper has heard other reports of duplicate tests being done . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The hospital has added extra staff to help with the transition and has brought in superusers to help in the early weeks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of those super users , Bassil Abuzeid , a recent Carleton University economics graduate who was trained to help support staff learning the new system , said he understands the transition is difficult . &amp;ldquo ; But from the superuser point of view , the system is not as bad as people think . It is meant to be more convenient for the staff . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Abuzeid said that in his experience staff members were catching on to the new system quickly . The result will be an improved information flow , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think the system creates transparency between the departments and is much more convenient . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The move to fully digitalize health records has been slow in Canada , with doctors offices and hospitals relying on faxes and handwritten notes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eggins , said some transition slowdowns were anticipated and the hospital has taken steps to minimize them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She also said a fully digital health records system is necessary . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The era of the fax machine has to go . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The doctor said physicians were given a chance to test the system and familiarize themselves with it ahead of time , but there were so many problems with that &amp;ldquo ; play space&amp;rdquo ; that they got little practice , which has left them feeling unprepared . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Under the new system , doctors and nurses are responsible for many of the tasks previously done by others &amp;mdash ; including scheduling and ordering medication . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some positions will been cut as a result , although the hospital says it will retrain people whose positions are made redundant . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The doctor conceded that some things are improving but said it feels like the complex new system has been rolled out too quickly and with too little support for those using it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The situation is discouraging and stressful for many physicians , who feel they are spending more time with the computer than with sick patients . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are really dedicated and invested in good patient care . This has really been a big blow to that . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;16935;TOOLONG " **34;16964;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;17000;TOOLONG ... OPP make arrest , seek second suspect in brutal weekend beating&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;17035;TOOLONG ... LeBreton Flats consultations take ' from the ground up ' approach&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;17070;TOOLONG ... builders commit to finish trains by July 7 , handing over working rail system in August&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Elizabeth PayneWed , 19 Jun 2019 10:28:19 **45;17105;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal NewsMenSeniorsWomenThe drug resistant fungus that is putting health officials on alerthttp : **35;17152;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;17189;TOOLONG ... title= " File photo . " alt= " File photo . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;17225;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1059346 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; A Winnipeg resident returned from India seriously ill with a brain abscess and a chronic ear infection . The unidentified patient , who had undergone dental surgery in India , was infected with a drug-resistant form of bacteria . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And that was not all . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The 64-year-old is now recognized as Canada&amp;rsquo ; s Patient Zero for an emerging new threat that has hospitals across the country on alert &amp;mdash ; multi-drug resistant Candida auris . The fungal infection was found in the patient&amp;rsquo ; s ear when &amp;ldquo ; cheesy&amp;rdquo ; fluid was drained from it and tested . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Candida auris can spread from patient to patient and can live on skin , walls , curtains , handles , cellphones and other surfaces . The risk of infection in otherwise healthy patients is relatively low ; Candida auris can colonize the skin of patients with no visible impact . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But in people with suppressed immune systems , it can cause a serious infection , enter their bloodstream and even be deadly . In some countries where it is common , mortality rates for those with invasive Candida auris are higher than 50 per cent . What is more , treatment options are limited or non-existent , and it is difficult to eradicate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And that is worrisome to hospitals , because many have significant populations of patients with suppressed immune systems &amp;mdash ; those undergoing cancer treatment or transplant patients , for example . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;17255;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;17297;TOOLONG ... technology sanitizes smart phones in hospitals with UV **26;17332;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; What has health officials most concerned about Candida auris is how difficult it is to identify , treat and contain &amp;mdash ; especially its persistence . A recent New York Times story described a hospital having to rip out floor and ceiling tiles in order to eradicate it from an infected patient&amp;rsquo ; s room . It can also live on reusable medical equipment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think it is something that all hospitals are worried about , &amp;rdquo ; said Dr. Julianne Kus , a clinical microbiologist at Public Health Ontario . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada has seen only 19 cases of Candida auris . Those cases include a cluster in British Columbia and five unrelated cases in southern Ontario . None of the Ontario cases was multi-drug resistant . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The authors of the paper about Patient Zero , who was diagnosed in May 2017 , note that multi-drug resistant Candida auris was first identified in Japan in 2009 and has , since then , become an emerging global threat . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The fact that it behaves differently than other infectious threats &amp;mdash ; even clinging to synthetic surfaces and forming a biofilm &amp;mdash ; makes it so hard to eradicate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Humans are colonized with many strains of Candida , notes Kus . Sometimes , they can cause infections such as vaginal yeast infections and athlete&amp;rsquo ; s foot that are more a nuisance than serious infections . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What makes Candida auris different , though , is that it can be spread from person to person , similar to bacteria and viruses . &amp;ldquo ; Typically , we have never really been concerned about fungi or yeast spreading from person to person , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What is more , Candida auris appears to be able to spread from a person into the environment and back to a person . When the strain is multi-drug resistant and resistant to disinfectant cleaning products , it quickly can become a nightmare for hospitals . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It seems to be acting a little bit differently . We haven&amp;rsquo ; t really thought about fungi this way before , &amp;rdquo ; said Kus. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another complicating factor is that treatment for fungal infections is limited and anti-fungal drugs can be harmful so must be used judiciously . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Earlier this year , Public Health Ontario published a document guiding health officials in the province on how to approach the emerging health threat . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among other advice : Rooms of patients or residents colonized with C. auris should be cleaned and disinfected daily , or even twice a day and medical equipment used on the patient should not be used on anyone else . C. auris can spread between patients as quickly as four hours and patients can even catch it from being in a room previously occupied by a patient . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The verdict is still out on the impact of ultraviolet light and other disinfectant procedures . There is some evidence that hydrogen peroxide vapour and ultraviolet light can reduce levels of environmental contamination with C. auris , but whether this will result in reduced transmission has not been confirmed , said Kus. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Facilities that are using ultraviolet light or hydrogen peroxide vapour should prioritize rooms housing C. auris patients for the disinfection , but the room should first be cleaned and disinfected using standard procedures , she said , which includes the use of bleach . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There have been no cases in Ottawa , but hospitals are prepared for cases . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kus says it is important to draw attention to multi-disease resistant infections such as Candida auris. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It highlights that we are still vulnerable to infectious disease . We will not be able to rely on antibiotics forever . &amp;rdquo ; Kus noted that the World Health Organization has named drug resistance as a leading health threat . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Should ( Canadians ) be worried ? In Canada we are doing a pretty good job so far in containing it . We haven&amp;rsquo ; t had the same experience as is happening in the States . I would say from the community level , I would not worry about it . But it is a good thing to talk about . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;17360;TOOLONG " **34;17389;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;17425;TOOLONG ... dog kills wild turkey downtown&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **34;17460;TOOLONG ... erupts as LeBlanc sentenced to 15 years for second-degree murder&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;17496;TOOLONG ... River expected to peak for a second time in May -- and it could be worse , officials warn&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Elizabeth PayneWed , 24 Apr 2019 11:42:51 **45;17531;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal NewsMenSeniorsWomenNew technology sanitizes smart phones in hospitals with UV lighthttp : **35;17578;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;17615;TOOLONG ... title= " The Montfort Hospital is using a CleanSlate UV machine to kill bacteria on devices like cellphones. " alt= " The Montfort Hospital is using a CleanSlate UV machine to kill bacteria on devices like cellphones. " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;17651;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1384996 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;17681;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Montfort Hospital features a microwave-like device in its front lobby that cleans your phone of dangerous bacteria using ultraviolet light . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The device , called CleanSlate UV , was created in order to combat antibiotic resistant bacteria born in hospitals and commonly referred to as superbugs . The illnesses they cause vary from minor infections to severe diseases . Common strains include C. difficile and MRSA. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; MRSA complications are sometimes as minor as small skin infections or as serious as pneumonia . Similarly , C. difficile can result in symptoms ranging from diarrhea to life-threatening inflammation of the colon . Both bacteria can live for weeks on hard surfaces , such as cellphones or tablets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By harnessing a specific wavelength of ultra-violet light that is refracted in the chamber of the device , CleanSlate UV attacks bacteria on mobile phones and other electronic devices so that it can no longer reproduce . The process takes 20 seconds &amp;ndash ; allowing time to use the hand-sanitizer located conveniently next to it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There have been numerous studies that show that one in four mobile hospital devices will have a pathogen bacteria on it , &amp;rdquo ; said Taylor Mann , co-founder of CleanSlate UV. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is a growing concern , as the number of mobile devices used in hospitals is steadily increasing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Hospitals put a huge focus on hand hygiene , &amp;rdquo ; Mann explained . &amp;ldquo ; But it doesn&amp;rsquo ; t make sense to wash your hands and then immediately touch a dirty device . Hospitals are deploying these very expensive touch-screen products to make their routine easier . &amp;rdquo ; Sanitizing the mobile devices is often not thought of until after they are purchased . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;17717;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;17759;TOOLONG ... drug resistant fungus that is putting health officials on **26;17794;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; The CleanSlate UV provides a non-chemical way of killing bacteria . Chemical disinfectants that are typically used to clean mobile devices are harsh , he said , and can wear devices down over time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It doesn&amp;rsquo ; t actually physically polish the device , &amp;rdquo ; Mann explained . &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re able to eliminate bacteria on the surfaces without damaging devices , and in a way that completely removes human error . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Montfort partnered with CleanSlate UV as part of a two-phase pilot project to conduct research on how the public can use CleanSlate. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We want to make it very quick , very easy , for the public to sanitize their devices at the same time as they are sanitizing their hands , &amp;rdquo ; Mann said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jos&amp;eacute ; e Shymanski , manager of the Infection Control Department at Montfort , said she&amp;rsquo ; s happy to see an increase in people washing their hands when they use the device . The CleanSlate&amp;rsquo ; s most recent location , by the line for the coffee shop in the front lobby , saw the most public usage . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We saw an increase to 32 times a day , &amp;rdquo ; she said , as compared to the 20 times a day it was used when it was located by the front entrance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The second phase of the pilot project involves technology that is not yet on the market . By putting small identification tags on hospital devices , the CleanSlate automatically records which devices are cleaned and when . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It all adds up to them having more information so that they can protect their staff and patients , &amp;rdquo ; Mann said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mann expects that the tagging technology will be available to purchase sometime this summer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;17822;TOOLONG ... flood of 2019 : ' Severe flood levels ' now expected&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;17857;TOOLONG ... Transpo retires route number in Westboro crash&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;17892;TOOLONG ... Obama is coming to speak in Ottawa&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Erica EndemannWed , 24 Apr 2019 11:40:03 **45;17927;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal **26;17974;TOOLONG policies in Canada and beyond : What 's working and what needs to changehttp : **34;18002;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;18038;TOOLONG ... title= " Dr. Zachary Kaminsky , the chair of suicide prevention , poses for a photo at the Royal in Ottawa Tuesday Feb 6 , 2018. " alt= " Dr. Zachary Kaminsky , the chair of suicide prevention , poses for a photo at the Royal in Ottawa Tuesday Feb 6 , 2018. " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;18074;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1382531 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Between 70 and 80 people take their own lives in Ottawa every year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. Simon Hatcher , a psychiatrist and researcher in clinical epidemiology at The Ottawa Hospital Research Institute , said he believes it&amp;rsquo ; s possible to reduce that number by 20 per cent within five years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s been done before . It&amp;rsquo ; s not rocket science , &amp;rdquo ; he says , pointing to the example of the Nuremberg model , a protocol first used in Germany which has now spread all over Europe and beyond . &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m sure it can be reduced . We can do better . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Suicide is considered a public health issue . But Ottawa Public Health has not set a target for reducing suicide in the city . Even the issue of setting targets is controversial . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m not sure setting a target will help . What will help is setting a collaborative approach , &amp;rdquo ; said Ben Leikin , supervisor of Ottawa Public Health&amp;rsquo ; s mental health team and co-chair of Suicide Prevention Ottawa , which recently merged with the Ottawa Suicide Prevention Coalition . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s also important to consider whether a reduction in the number of suicide deaths and attempts would continue over time . There are some environmental factors , such as natural disasters or large-scale layoffs for example , that can&amp;rsquo ; t be controlled and have an effect on the suicide rate , says Leikin. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Leikin said while he applauds those who have set a target for suicide reduction , Ottawa has not . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Any evidence that exists out there , we&amp;rsquo ; re open to it . Let&amp;rsquo ; s not reinvent the wheel . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There have been a number of strategies touted to prevent suicide . Here are a few of them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;18104;TOOLONG ... title= " Psychologist Dr. Antoon Leenaars. " alt= " Psychologist Dr. Antoon Leenaars. " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;18140;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1382530 " /&gt; **28;18170;TOOLONG **33;18200;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;What is it ? &lt;/strong&gt; This &amp;ldquo ; continuous improvement model , &amp;rdquo ; first implemented in health-care systems in the U.S. , works on the idea that people who have contact with the health-care system can be prevented from taking their own lives . There are three main pillars : a direct approach to suicidal behaviours , continual improvement of the quality and safety of care processes and an organizational commitment to the aspirational goal of zero suicides. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among the measures : removing the lethal means of suicide ; training a competent and caring workforce ; using screening to determine who is at risk of suicide ; ensuring all patients at risk are closely followed ; treating suicidal ideation independent of the patient&amp;rsquo ; s diagnosis and transitioning patients with &amp;ldquo ; warm hand-offs and supportive contact . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Does it work ? &lt;/strong&gt; The Henry Ford Health System , a non-profit health-care provider in the Detroit area that includes eight hospitals , 48 medical centres and 13 walk-in clinics , has often been named as an example of good outcomes . Henry Ford , which calls the protocol &amp;ldquo ; perfect depression care , &amp;rdquo ; introduced it in 2001 . In a study published in 2007 , Henry Ford reported a 75 per cent reduction in the suicide rate . There were actually zero suicides in the Henry Ford system for an 18-month stretch between 2009-2010. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The model has moved into Europe and Canada . The first Zero Suicide campaign in Canada was unveiled in May 2016 at the St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s Healthcare Foundation in London , Ont. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The model is not without controversy in the mental health community . Hatcher says there is one major problem with translating the zero suicide model : three-quarters of people who die by suicide have no contact with mental health services in the year leading to their death . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s a lot of rhetoric about it , but not a lot of evidence , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; It irritated a lot of clinicians by calling it Zero Suicide . It&amp;rsquo ; s like going into intensive care and saying , &amp;lsquo ; There will be zero deaths . &amp;rsquo ; As a psychiatrist , I am responsible for getting ( my patients ) the best treatment possible . The implication here is that we are responsible for a death by suicide . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Zero Suicide works for hospitals and health-care networks , not for regions and jurisdictions , said Leikin. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is also debate over whether zero is an attainable goal . Zero Suicide calls it an &amp;ldquo ; aspirational challenge and practical framework for system-wide transformation . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think we should shoot for Zero Suicide . But it will never happen . There will always be people with unbearable angst . Our task is to work toward it , &amp;rdquo ; says psychologist Dr. Antoon Leenaars , the former president of the Canadian Association for Suicide Prevention . &lt;/p&gt; **27;18235;TOOLONG Nuremberg **31;18264;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;What is it ? &lt;/strong&gt; The Nuremberg Alliance Against Depression was initiated in 2001 as a community-based model project in the German city of Nuremberg , which has about 500,000 residents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;How does it work ? &lt;/strong&gt; Called a &amp;ldquo ; multi-level intervention , &amp;rdquo ; it&amp;rsquo ; s a four-level approach that engages partners from parents to police officers to family physicians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; First level : General practitioners and pediatricians are invited to educational workshops on how to recognize and treat depression in the primary care setting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Second level : An awareness campaign for the general public is aimed at improving knowledge and reducing stigma . Campaigns have included posters , advertising spots in movie theatres , brochures , public events and websites . Local organizers are also urged to develop a relationship with the media to prompt discussion , and to set guidelines on coverage to prevent copycat suicides. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Third level : Patients in high risk groups &amp;mdash ; such as teens in crisis &amp;mdash ; are given &amp;ldquo ; emergency cards&amp;rdquo ; guaranteeing direct access to professional help . Local self-help groups are provided with expert advice and partnerships are created with patient associations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fourth level : Workshops are held with groups such as health-care professionals , counsellors , clergy and police officers . There are also information sessions for parents , teachers and youth workers and prevention programs in schools . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The program also tracks data on &amp;ldquo ; core indicators&amp;rdquo ; such as number of suicides , number of suicide attempts , public opinion , attitudes , knowledge and media coverage of suicides. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Does it work ? &lt;/strong&gt; A lot of other jurisdictions have adopted it . After suicidal behaviour was reduced by 20 per cent in Nuremberg , 18 partners in 17 European countries established the &lt;a href= " http : //www.eaad.net/ " &gt;European Alliance Against Depression&lt;/a&gt; in 2004 . There are now about 100 regional networks in the alliance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Mental Health Commission of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s &lt;a href= " https : **33;18297;TOOLONG ... of Hope&lt;/a&gt; suicide prevention program , which has been adopted by communities in Labrador , Newfoundland and New Brunswick , has some parallels , but the European Alliance has stricter protocols . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;18332;TOOLONG ... title= " NDP MP Charlie Angus stands in the House of Commons during question period on Parliament Hill in Ottawa on June 9 , 2015. " alt= " NDP MP Charlie Angus stands in the House of Commons during question period on Parliament Hill in Ottawa on June 9 , 2015. " data-portal-copyright= " FRED CHARTRAND " **28;18368;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 794461 " /&gt; &lt;div class= " mceTemp " &gt;&lt;/div&gt; **25;18398;TOOLONG national **34;18425;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;What is it ? &lt;/strong&gt; More than 20 countries have a national strategy for suicide prevention . Canada has a federal framework , but not a strategy , and that needs to change , argues the Centre for Suicide Prevention , a branch of the Canadian Mental Health Association . The Canadian Association for Suicide Prevention has also been calling for a national strategy for more than 20 years and &lt;a href= " https : **31;18461;TOOLONG " &; ... a blueprint&lt;/a&gt; in 2004 and an update in 2009. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada is the only G8 country without a national suicide prevention strategy and one of the few industrialized countries that doesn&amp;rsquo ; t have one , according to the Centre for Suicide Prevention . The U.S. , Australia , Japan , Scotland , England and Finland all have working national policies in place . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The centre says research shows that a strategy must address suicide directly and have its own prevention strategy , outside the broad umbrella of mental health , &amp;ldquo ; otherwise there is a danger that the prevention message will be diluted , ignored , or lost altogether . &amp;rdquo ; The strategy also can&amp;rsquo ; t be the responsibility of a single government department , but must be addressed by all government departments and sectors affected , including the community , workplace , schools , families , the criminal justice system and education . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Does it work ? &lt;/strong&gt; Finland was one of the first countries to introduce a national strategy in the 1980s , and the suicide rate there dropped by nine per cent over 10 years , according to the Centre for Suicide Prevention . Scotland introduced a strategy in 2002 and saw an 18 per cent reduction in suicides by 2012. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jackie Doyle-Price was named the United Kingdom&amp;rsquo ; s first Suicide Prevention Minister in October 2018 . Her role oversees a strategy that includes a focus on how social media and technology , such as predictive analytics and artificial intelligence , can help identify suicide risk . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; England&amp;rsquo ; s strategy was introduced in 2012 . In a progress report published in January , Doyle-Price said the suicide rate in England was at its lowest in seven years . &amp;ldquo ; However , these rates can change over time , &amp;rdquo ; she noted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Achieving a low rate and maintaining it over time is an eternal problem in suicide prevention . In the U.S. , which has a national strategy , the suicide rate increased 24 per cent between 1999 and 2014 , from 10.5 to 13 suicides for every 100,000 people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; NDP MP Charlie Angus introduced a parliamentary motion for a national suicide prevention &amp;ldquo ; action plan&amp;rdquo ; earlier this year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We need to put language to this that says this is an epidemic , that it&amp;rsquo ; s causing devastation , &amp;rdquo ; says Angus. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It also has to be a made-in-Canada plan that recognizes the realities of vulnerable groups in Canadian society , such as Indigenous Peoples , and the relationship between the provinces and federal government when it comes to health policy , he says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among other measures , &lt;a href= " https : **32;18494;TOOLONG ... motion , M-174&lt;/a&gt; , calls for a national public health monitoring program , national standards of training for those who work with vulnerable populations and an annual report to Parliament , which would include data on progress for the previous year . &amp;ldquo ; If we&amp;rsquo ; re not diminishing the numbers of deaths , then the plan is not working , &amp;rdquo ; said Angus. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The motion is up for second debate on May 2 . If it passes , Angus said Canada would be obligated to put a number of steps in place , including identifying groups at elevated risk and introducing strategies that would prevent suicide among these groups within 18 months . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was shocked at the level of suicide among middle-aged men , &amp;rdquo ; said Angus. &amp;ldquo ; When a large industrial plant goes down , we need to have a plan to raise awareness ( about suicide ) . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; **37;18528;TOOLONG the biology of **43;18567;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;What is it ? &lt;/strong&gt; Scientists are delving into the complicated interplay between genetics and environment and the role each plays in suicide risk . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For example , molecular biologist Zachary Kaminsky&amp;rsquo ; s research hit the news in 2014 after he and colleagues at Johns Hopkins University in Baltimore discovered a chemical alteration in a gene called SKA2 linked to stress reduction . The finding suggested that doctors could identify suicide risk and even prevent suicide through a blood or saliva test . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But it&amp;rsquo ; s complicated , said Kaminsky , who is now the DIFD Mach-Gaensslen Chair in Suicide Prevention Research at The Royal&amp;rsquo ; s Institute of Mental Health Research . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kaminsky did a study of about 925 soldiers deployed to Afghanistan . A blood test identified a variant of SKA2 , correctly identifying five soldiers who had elevated scores for suicidal ideation. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But the tests also found a high number of false positives &amp;mdash ; these soldiers tested positive , but they did not have suicidal thoughts . That brings up the question of identifying people who are not at risk . There&amp;rsquo ; s also the problem of identifying those who are at risk , but not being able to do anything to help them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have to understand if we will cause a problem that we have no solution for , &amp;rdquo ; said Kaminsky. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Does it work ? &lt;/strong&gt; Researchers are learning more and more about the interplay for genetics and the environment . But it&amp;rsquo ; s not as simple as administering a once-in-a-lifetime blood test to everyone to determine whether or not an individual is genetically vulnerable to suicidal thoughts . First of all , genes may be switched on or off by traumatic or stressful events . Biological systems are adaptive to experiences . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s not just genes . It&amp;rsquo ; s context , &amp;rdquo ; said Kaminsky. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the same time , vulnerability to suicide risk is believed to be determined by a complex interplay of genetic factors . &amp;ldquo ; Is there one gene that does it all ? If I said that , I would get ripped apart , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; In term of coming up with a magic bullet that works for everyone , it becomes very challenging . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But he also has a lot of hope . At some point , a negative test result may conclude that someone is not at risk of suicide , for example . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The more research we do , the more we understand about the biological systems that will lead to more treatment targets , &amp;rdquo ; said Kaminsky. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Treatments target systems we believe to be dysregulated with the hope of bringing them back in line . The more we know , the more we will be able to do this correctly . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He uses an analogy : A car owner notices the coolant is low , so he adds more . Then it runs low again , so he adds more . Adding more solved the problem for a time , but not the underlying problem . The mechanic figures out that the problem is the water pump . &amp;ldquo ; Once fixed , no more symptoms and no more treating the symptoms because the treatment was better . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are also promising new drug treatments for helping people in crisis . In March , the FDA approved a new depression treatment in the form of ketamine , which has been used as a painkiller , anesthetic and recreational drug , delivered as a nasal spray . A study of depressed patients at imminent risk of suicide in emergency rooms at 11 centres in the U.S. found the treatment resulted in significantly rapid improvement in depressive symptoms &amp;mdash ; including some measures of suicidal ideation. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt; &lt;strong&gt;FEATURE STORY&lt;/strong&gt; : &lt;a href= " https : **33;18612;TOOLONG ... Richards took her own life at 26 . She left behind devastated loved ones and a haunting question&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Joanne LauciusThu , 18 Apr 2019 13:07:06 **45;18647;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal **25;18694;TOOLONG Hospital team pioneers virtual reality to treat Parkinson 's patientshttp : **32;18721;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;18755;TOOLONG ... title= " Medical physicist Justin Sutherland , left , explains the virtual reality technology as neurosurgeon Dr. Adam Sachs virtually pokes around inside someone 's brain at The Ottawa Hospital 's General campus Monday , March 18 , 2019. " alt= " Medical physicist Justin Sutherland , left , explains the virtual reality technology as neurosurgeon Dr. Adam Sachs virtually pokes around inside someone 's brain at The Ottawa Hospital 's General campus Monday , March 18 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Julie Oliver " **28;18791;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1368473 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;18821;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; With a virtual reality goggles fixed on his head and wands in his hands , neurosurgeon Dr. Adam Sachs can step inside a patient&amp;rsquo ; s brain for an extreme close up of where he will place an electrode during deep brain stimulation surgery &amp;mdash ; virtually , that is. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For now , the cutting-edge technique , created at The Ottawa Hospital&amp;rsquo ; s virtual reality lab , is still in development . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Sachs , director of neuromodulation and functional neurosurgery at TOH and a researcher with the Ottawa Hospital Research Institute , expects to be using it in the operating room within months . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Virtual reality has been used in medicine for several years , mainly to help train physicians or to assist patients with rehabilitation , PTSD or other issues . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But it has never been used like this . The technique developed to improve the accuracy of the game-changing surgery for patients with Parkinson&amp;rsquo ; s and other neurological diseases represents a world first for The Ottawa Hospital . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Deep brain stimulation surgery involves implanting electrodes inside the brain connected to a stimulator device something like a pacemaker . Where the electrodes are placed determines the symptoms being treated . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;18857;TOOLONG ... title= " Dr. Adam Sachs virtually pokes around inside a virtual human brain at The Ottawa Hospital 's General campus Monday , March 18 , 2019 with the help of new virtual reality technology . The 3D Virtual Reality Lab at the hospital has innovated technology scheduled to be rolled out this year that is the first of its kind , allowing neurosurgeons to increase their accuracy during deep brain stimulation for patients . " alt= " Dr. Adam Sachs virtually pokes around inside a virtual human brain at The Ottawa Hospital 's General campus Monday , March 18 , 2019 with the help of new virtual reality technology . The 3D Virtual Reality Lab at the hospital has innovated technology scheduled to be rolled out this year that is the first of its kind , allowing neurosurgeons to increase their accuracy during deep brain stimulation for patients . " data-portal-copyright= " Julie Oliver " **28;18893;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1368472 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The surgery can help to reduce tremor , slowness , stiffness and walking problems caused by Parkinson&amp;rsquo ; s and other neurological diseases . It has the potential to not only improve mobility , but can decrease reliance on medications that can come with side effects . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It makes a remarkable difference in a patient&amp;rsquo ; s quality of life , &amp;rdquo ; said Sachs , who has been performing the surgery at The Ottawa Hospital for five years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Implanting a microelectrode that is no thicker than a human hair through the skull into a specific area of the brain is technical work . Surgeons use MRIs and brain atlases ( visual brain mapping ) to get a mental picture of what the patient&amp;rsquo ; s brain looks like . But the brain atlases are two-dimensional and made from a compilation of many patients&amp;rsquo ; brains . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That is why this virtual reality environment is so helpful , because it actually allows us to turn it into an intuitive , visual three-dimensional experience that can be used to more accurately place the electrode , as opposed to looking at the top of the skull and closing your eyes and trying to think , Where am I ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Using virtual reality helps to improve precision because surgeons are using accurate images from the patient&amp;rsquo ; s own brain in 3-D using MRIs and CT scans . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And precision is crucial when it comes to deep brain stimulation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are talking about a refinement of millimetres , &amp;rdquo ; said Sachs . &amp;ldquo ; But millimetres matter . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sachs has worked with Dr. Justin Sutherland and Dr. Daniel La Russa on the technique . Both are medical physicists in the hospital&amp;rsquo ; s radiation oncology department . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although virtual reality has been used for patients , &amp;ldquo ; doctors wearing the headsets instead of patients is fairly limited , largely because technology wasn&amp;rsquo ; t there in the past , &amp;rdquo ; said Sutherland . But that will change , he said , and could change the way medicine is practised in some cases . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sutherland envisions it being used throughout the hospital in coming years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For now , there is potential for the virtual reality technique to spread around the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As far as we know , no one else is pursuing this type of thing and it could have a global impact , &amp;rdquo ; Sutherland said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;18923;TOOLONG " **34;18952;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;18988;TOOLONG ... do Ottawa lawyers love LRT ? ' goosebuster ' pilot wishes he could start bullying birds before June&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Elizabeth PayneTue , 19 Mar 2019 10:51:43 **45;19093;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal NewsScienceSeniorsFormer LHIN chair says system brought innovation and cost savings to regional healthhttp : **34;19140;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;19176;TOOLONG ... title= " Jean-Pierre Boisclair , former chair of the Champlain LHIN , lost his job last week . " alt= " Jean-Pierre Boisclair , former chair of the Champlain LHIN , lost his job last week . " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;19212;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1367909 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ten days after losing his job as chair of the Champlain LHIN board , Jean-Pierre Boisclair is defending the LHIN system and questioning whether revamping health care will save Ontario money . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There is work to be done and somebody has to do it , regardless of how the system is organized , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Boisclair was one of 10 Champlain LHIN board members who received word on March 7 that their appointments would be revoked the following day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The move didn&amp;rsquo ; t surprise Boisclair. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; My sense was that the government wants to get on with their agenda and that was the most expeditious way they could go about it . I wasn&amp;rsquo ; t totally surprised , but it is always abrupt when you get a call in the afternoon and you are gone the next day . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The province announced a major overhaul of the health care system earlier this month . Government officials said the transition could take up to several years , which means the LHINs would stay in place for a while . Their boards , however , have been replaced by the 12-person Ontario Health Agency board appointed last week . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Boisclair , who has previously served on boards at The Ottawa Hospital and the Ottawa Children&amp;rsquo ; s Treatment Centre , chaired the Champlain LHIN board for four years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; During that time , the CCACs ( community care access centres ) , which run home care , were folded into LHINs , which means that every day , the Champlain LHIN oversees as many as 20,000 home-care patients , Boisclair noted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Whatever the final organization of health care looks like in the province , Boisclair said it is crucial that there be a smooth transition and that there is no gap in services for home-care patients . He said the LHIN has created a foundation on which components of the new health system can build . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Champlain LHIN currently has 850 employees , many of them care co-ordinators or others directly involved in home care . Boisclair said the LHIN has been modernizing the way home care is delivered to make the system more efficient . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ontario government has adopted several other innovations introduced by Champlain LHIN , he noted . They include an electronic specialist referral system that both shortened waitlists for patients to see specialists and reduced unnecessary visits to specialists by 40 per cent . The province is also implementing a central intake for hip and knee surgery that allows candidates to take the first available surgeon . Champlain region went from the longest wait times for hips and knees in the province to the shortest . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Boisclair said the role of the LHIN in those and other examples was to act as an &amp;ldquo ; air traffic controller&amp;rdquo ; for all the health services in the region . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You need air traffic control at a local level as well as a system-wide level or chaos is going to ensue . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The health care reforms involve the creation of a super agency that will take over for 20 provincial health agencies &amp;mdash ; including 14 regional LHINs . Front-line health care is to be provided by integrated care teams made up of hospitals and other health-care organizations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Boisclair predicted such integration will be complicated . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Integration doesn&amp;rsquo ; t always happen naturally . Organic growth is not necessarily an easy thing to accomplish , &amp;rdquo ; he said , adding that health care providers are independent operators who &amp;ldquo ; have their own agendas&amp;rdquo ; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Boisclair said he was saddened to hear Premier Doug Ford take a shot at the salaries of LHIN executives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The LHIN executives earn their keep in no uncertain terms . They are a very qualified group of people . There is work that has to be done no matter what and it takes competitive people and the right number , otherwise funny things start to happen . &amp;ldquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;19242;TOOLONG " **34;19271;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;19307;TOOLONG ... do Ottawa lawyers love LRT ? &lt;/p&gt; Elizabeth PayneMon , 18 Mar 2019 11:11:37 **45;19412;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal NewsSeniorsOntario drafting plans to create super agency to run health carehttp : **36;19459;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;19497;TOOLONG ... title= " Hospital crowding " alt= " Hospital crowding " data-portal-copyright= " John Mahoney file photo " **28;19533;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 713638 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ontario needs a more centralized and integrated health-care system as it copes with growing demographic challenges , a premier&amp;rsquo ; s council charged with ending hallway medicine says in its first report . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The initial report from the council headed by retired hospital CEO Dr. Reuben Devlin was released Thursday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It doesn&amp;rsquo ; t contain specific recommendations , but sketches a future of health care in Ontario that likely does not include the current regionalized system of LHINs ( Local Health Integration Networks ) , that is leaner and that is centrally controlled . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt; **28;19679;TOOLONG lang= " en-US " &gt;Here are the findings of the report &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span lang= " en-US " &gt;Hallway Health Care : A system under strain : **35;19709;TOOLONG &lt;ul&gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;span lang= " en-US " &gt;&lt;span lang= " en-US " &gt;1,000 patients a day were waiting for a hospital bed on an stretcher or unconventional space in 2018 ; **37;19746;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt; &lt;span lang= " en-US " &gt;&lt;span lang= " en-US " &gt;Visits to emergency departments increased by 11 per cent over the past six years ; **26;19785;TOOLONG &lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;Patients who require admission are spending an average of 16 hours in the emergency department before a bed is available ; &lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;The median wait for long-term care was 146 days in 2017/18 ; &lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;There has been a 72 per cent increase in emergency department visits and a 79 per cent increase in in-patient admissions for children and youth with mental health issues over past 11 years . &lt;/li&gt; &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s current health-care system can be characterized as decentralized , large and siloed , and it can be difficult at times to know who is responsible and accountable for ensuring Ontarians have access to high-value health care , &amp;rdquo ; the report says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Stronger lines of accountability would help make the health-care system more efficient and also help ensure Ontario gets a greater value for what it currently spends on health care . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19813;TOOLONG ... title= " Stethoscopes. " alt= " Stethoscopes. " data-portal-copyright= " Khalil Hamra " **28;19849;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1299279 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The report also notes that there are 21 health-related government agencies supporting the design and delivery of health care in Ontario . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Many of these agencies were created to tackle specific problems , support research , or to establish quality standards and metrics to help the system as it matured . However , these agencies are not always well aligned and there is limited strategic oversight to ensure the efficient and co-ordinated use of resources . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The council is widely expected to kill the regional LHIN system and to replace it with a super agency in charge of everything from Public Health Ontario and the Trillium Gift of Life Network to Cancer Care Ontario . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Critics , including former deputy minister of health Dr. Bob Bell , have warned that such a super agency could decrease the quality of health care in the province . A similar centralization in British Columbia worsened cancer care in the province , Bell said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ottawa area&amp;rsquo ; s Champlain LHIN gets praise from Bell and others for achievements in co-ordinating and making health care more efficient in the region , but the LHIN system has many critics who call it unnecessarily cumbersome and bureaucratic and who say it was never given the tools to make a real difference . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19879;TOOLONG ... title= " Emergency room . " alt= " Emergency room . " data-portal-copyright= " Kelly Roche " **28;19915;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1124985 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The interim report spells out many of the well-documented challenges with health care in the province , notably overcrowded hospitals , patients waiting in hospital beds because there is not more suitable care available and the strain on the long-term care system . There are currently 72 long-term care beds per 1,000 people aged 75 or older in the province , a number that is projected to decline to 29 beds per 1,000 people over 75 by 2041 &amp;mdash ; a decline of 60 per cent or the equivalent of 48,000 bed closures &amp;ldquo ; if nothing is done , &amp;rdquo ; says the report . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It acknowledges that Ontario has the lowest per capital spending on health care compared to other provinces and territories , but adds : &amp;ldquo ; The system could work smarter and use this same amount of money to achieve better health outcomes . &amp;rdquo ; Canada , compared to some other countries , spends more but scores lower on some performance indicators. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The solutions to the issues outlined in the report are what worry observers and critics . Although there are bed shortages , simply adding more beds is not the solution , the report says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bell has been vocal about his concerns that rapid and massive changes could degrade Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s many health care successes , including cancer care . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sudbury MPP France G&amp;eacute ; linas , the NDP health critic , fears the changes will open the door to more privatization of health care across the province , and less input from regions &amp;mdash ; especially northern Ontario . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The province is likely to be divided into five health regions , including one that includes Ottawa and encompasses most of the province east of the GTA . The northern region would include everything north of the French River . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The people who are the most excited about this are the big private healthcare providers , &amp;rdquo ; said G&amp;eacute ; linas. &amp;ldquo ; They will sell a model that is glossy that further opens the door to care based on the ability to pay . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa Hospital CEO Dr. Jack Kitts is among 14 members of the Premier&amp;rsquo ; s Council on Improving Health Care and Ending Hallway Medicine . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;19945;TOOLONG ... launched in support of bus crash survivor after double amputation&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;19980;TOOLONG ... who hear more than one language at home develop attention advantage : study&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;20015;TOOLONG ... &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; A Carleton University researcher has invented a high-speed X-ray system that&amp;rsquo ; s now being used to study the effects of collisions on football and hockey helmets , pig brains , synthetic brains and cadavers. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Prof. Oren Petel conducts his research from a small , lead-lined lab on the sixth floor of Carleton&amp;rsquo ; s Canal Building . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The lab is equipped with a linear impactor , a machine&amp;nbsp ; capable of delivering a head hit at 12 metres per second , along with an X-ray system that can capture images of the collision at 100,000 frames per second . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It means Petel and his research team can recreate in spectacular detail what happens to a helmet &amp;mdash ; and to a brain &amp;mdash ; during a high-speed collision . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Instead of looking just at how the head is moving , we can visualize what&amp;rsquo ; s happening to a representative brain model inside the head , &amp;rdquo ; said Petel , a professor in mechanical and aerospace engineering . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The machine&amp;rsquo ; s high-resolution images are used to create a video &amp;mdash ; the process is known as cineradiography &amp;mdash ; that shows the interaction between a helmet and a head while also revealing the brain&amp;rsquo ; s physical response to an impact : how it compresses , twists and stretches . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Taken together , Petel said , the information promises to inform the work of researchers as they design better helmets for hockey and football players , soldiers and cyclists . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s next-level information that can be used to validate the computer models used in helmet design , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;20268;TOOLONG ... title= " Here , he sets up a machine that hits the " head " with great velocity from which he can measure impact and damage to the head . " alt= " Here , he sets up a machine that hits the " head " with great velocity from which he can measure impact and damage to the head . " data-portal-copyright= " Julie Oliver " **28;20304;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1340589 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Petel has submitted patent applications for his test methodology and his imaging system , which is among the fastest in the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The system became operational in the summer of 2017 , and is now being deployed in a number of concussion and helmet research projects . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One project , being conducted in partnership with Defence Research and Development Canada , is testing what happens to a synthetic brain inside a plastic head model . Markers are implanted inside the artificial brain to measure how it deforms in a collision . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another research project , being pursued with Carleton neuroscience professor Matt Holahan , studies how pig brains ( collected from an abattoir ) respond to an impact . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re trying to understand the specific injuries that the cells undergo when the brain stretches , &amp;rdquo ; explained Petel. &amp;ldquo ; We want to know how the stretch and pull response of the brain might lead to injury and , specifically , where those injuries might be occurring . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We want to understand the critical limits of what those cells and those structures can take before they&amp;rsquo ; re affected . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still another project seeks to validate the computer models now commonly used in head injury research . Those models take information from impact experiments and predict how the brain will respond to the forces involved . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Working with researchers from the University of Ottawa , &amp;nbsp ; Petel&amp;rsquo ; s lab is using cadavers to test the computer models , and to add information about how regions deep in the brain are affected . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The brain is a very complex system and understanding exactly what leads to injury is not a trivial matter . We&amp;rsquo ; re still developing that understanding , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Petel took an unusual path into concussion research . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Born and raised in Montreal , he developed an early interest in physics and decided to study mechanical engineering . At McGill University , he joined the Shockwave Physics Group , where he immersed himself in blast research . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;20334;TOOLONG ... title= " Folds in a human brain . " alt= " Folds in a human brain . " data-portal-copyright= " David Duprey " **28;20370;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1039817 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It just sounded like a lot of fun , things impacting each other , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; And it was really challenging . Your experiments typically last several microseconds , several millionths of a second , and you have to collect all your data in the time before your experiment is destroyed . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As he studied the response of ballistic armour and other protective materials to an explosion , Petel became more and more interested in the dynamics of blast injuries . What was happening internally when a body was hit by a shock wave ? What made the lungs and brain so susceptible to injury ? How was the tissue deforming ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Petel felt that better answers to those questions could improve the design of protective equipment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He began to study injury biomechanics , and , at one conference , he asked a seasoned researcher why there wasn&amp;rsquo ; t more information about the reaction of internal tissue to a blast . He was told there was no way to measure what happened at such high speeds . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; So why isn&amp;rsquo ; t someone developing something to measure this ? &amp;rdquo ; Petel asked . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The researcher bristled : &amp;ldquo ; If you think you&amp;rsquo ; re so smart , why don&amp;rsquo ; t you do it ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A PhD student at the time , Petel took up that challenge . In December 2010 , he launched a private company to attract investment for the development of a high-speed X-ray system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But commercial investors were unwilling to take a chance on an unproven student , so Petel set his sights on becoming a professor . He figured it was the only way to pursue his idea . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After an exhaustive search , he secured a job in Carleton University&amp;rsquo ; s Department of Mechanical and Aerospace Engineering in August 2013 , and applied for funding to develop his X-ray system and an impact research lab. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canada Foundation for Innovation and the Ontario Research Fund financed most of the project , which cost about $310,000 and took more than two years to complete . The Canadian Institutes of Health Research and the Natural Sciences and Engineering Research Council have contributed to the lab&amp;rsquo ; s ongoing research . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Concussion research has come a long way during the past decade , Petel said , but more needs to be done to understand what happens inside the brain during a high-speed collision and how to design a helmet to better protect against injury . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He hopes his research will ultimately make it safer for athletes to compete in sports such as hockey and football . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The ultimate goal of this research is to change the way helmets are designed to improve helmet response , and also to influence in the longer term how helmets are evaluated and the safety standards applied to them . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;20400;TOOLONG " **34;20429;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;20465;TOOLONG ... border agent developed appetite for child porn after exposure at work&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;20500;TOOLONG ... remembrance cast against Hitler plan to purge Canadian Jews&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;20535;TOOLONG ... researchers warn that lack of sleep is a public health crisis&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Andrew DuffyMon , 28 Jan 2019 14:31:26 **45;20570;TOOLONG ... **27;20617;TOOLONG up support : Pain doctor fears treatment options could be restrictedhttp : **30;20646;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;20678;TOOLONG ... title= " Derek Debeer , former drummer with the band Johnny Clegg and Suvuka , who has received treatment from Dr. Ellen Thompson for back and arm pain , which plagues many professional drummers. " alt= " Derek Debeer , former drummer with the band Johnny Clegg and Suvuka , who has received treatment from Dr. Ellen Thompson for back and arm pain , which plagues many professional drummers. " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;20714;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1340270 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Like many professional drummers , Derek De Beer paid a price for the job he loved . There was a time when the former drummer with the South African band Savuka lived with excruciating back and elbow pain that threatened to end his drumming days . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; De Beer , who has lived in Ottawa for 30 years , searched in vain for help before meeting pain specialist Dr. Ellen Thompson . She injected small amounts of local anaesthetic agents and cortisone into trigger points in the muscles around his painful joints . Attacks every year or two were treated with consistent pain relief . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The treatment worked . It allowed&amp;nbsp ; De Beer to get on with his life and work , which includes teaching drumming and using drumming in his corporate team-building business . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I play drums . I teach a lot and carry a lot . I am pretty hard on my body and it takes a toll , &amp;rdquo ; he says . Still , after having a number of treatments , De Beer has not needed the treatment for five years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thompson said she has seen good results from the treatment in patients in severe pain and research backs up its effectiveness . But she fears that changes coming in Ontario could limit the ability of doctors to offer the inexpensive and effective treatment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;20744;TOOLONG ... title= " Derek Debeer " alt= " Derek Debeer " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;20780;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1340271 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; At a time when steps are being taken to limit opioid prescribing for patients with chronic pain , the injections &amp;ldquo ; represent a major , although limited , period of relief from pain , &amp;rdquo ; Thompson said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Most chronic pain patients state that the degree of relief from local anaesthetic injections is far more profound than the relief provided by even powerful opioids. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thompson said she is worried that such injections will be restricted after concerns were raised by the Ministry of Health about the increased use of nerve blocks and other similar injection procedures in the province between 2009 and 2015 . It has been the subject of discussions between the Ontario Medical Association and the province in ongoing negotiations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are reasons why the government may wish to put restrictions on frequency of the injections , Thompson said . Some practitioners are performing a large number of injections on patients per visit . &amp;ldquo ; Having watched some of them in action , it is clear that accuracy and precision is sacrificed to speed and a larger number of injections being performed . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thompson said some practitioners have been billing OHIP hundreds of thousands of dollar a year through performing dozens of injections per visit . There is a fee for each nerve that is injected . The fees for trigger points are less than $20 , she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is a need for more sensible methods of paying pain physicians , said Thompson . &amp;ldquo ; But this must be achieved without further jeopardizing pain patients&amp;rsquo ; ability to function . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thompson has previously raised concerns that it has become more difficult to effectively treat chronic pain because of crackdowns on prescribing opioids . The growth in injections could reflect physicians searching for chronic pain relief for their patients without relying on opioids. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Twenty per cent of the population suffers from chronic pain , she noted and treatments that allow sufferers to work , sleep and function are crucial . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thompson said long-lasting chronic pain can often be prevented if the injections are administered in the first few weeks after the onset of pain . For patients with ongoing chronic pain , the treatment can allow them to get some relief to sleep , work and live their lives , she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Back pain is one of the most expensive problems in all first world countries . No one is treating patients optimally to prevent chronic pain anywhere . We have the skill and knowledge . We need to start using it , even if it will remove a good source of steady income for a large number of clinics and their private investors . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Recent studies have questioned costly spinal fusion surgery that is sometimes offered as a last resort option for back pain patients , finding it is no more effective than non-surgical interventions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Neil Teitelbaum visited 25 practitioners for help with chronic pain before being treated by Thompson with trigger point injections . Teitelbaum , an electrical and computer engineer , said the treatment left him pain free for three to four years , the most effective relief he has had . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; De Beer , meanwhile , said he recommends the treatment to friends with pain problems and has been tempted to contact well-known drummers , including Phil Collins and Neil Peart of Rush , who are both known to suffer from pain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; David Jensen , spokesman for the Ministry of Health , said the ministry supports an &amp;ldquo ; evidence-based approach&amp;rdquo ; to managing chronic pain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Nerve blocks have a role in the management of patients with chronic pain but would rarely be the sole modality of treatment . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Any change to OHIP services or payment formulas , he added , would involve consultations with the Ontario Medical Association and be subject to the current binding arbitration process . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;20810;TOOLONG ... of Ottawa readies test track in Nepean for autonomous vehicle technologies&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;20845;TOOLONG ... assault victim asks court to lift publication ban in her case&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;20880;TOOLONG ... a grip on icy falls in Lanark County&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Elizabeth PayneFri , 25 Jan 2019 14:21:43 **45;20915;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal **25;20962;TOOLONG sending emergency management expert to help fight Ebola in West Africahttp : **34;20989;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;21025;TOOLONG ... title= " In this Sunday , Sept 9 , 2018 file photo , a health worker sprays disinfectant on his colleague after working at an Ebola treatment centre in Beni , Eastern Congo . " data-portal-copyright= " Al-hadji Kudra Maliro " **28;21061;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1332288 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canadian government has sent an emergency management expert to the Republic of the Congo to help the World Health Organization battle the worsening Ebola outbreak in nearby Democratic Republic of Congo . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The outbreak is now the second largest in history after the West Africa Ebola one that killed 11,000 people between 2014 and 2016 and put the world on edge . So far , 385 people have died in DRC and the outbreak , amid ongoing conflict and contentious elections , continues to grow . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Public Health Agency of Canada is &amp;ldquo ; closely monitoring the situation , &amp;rdquo ; said spokeswoman Anna Maddison . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although there are new tools to fight Ebola since the 2014 crisis &amp;mdash ; notably the Canadian-developed experimental vaccine &amp;mdash ; the backdrop of political instability and violence in DRC is making it difficult to contain the outbreak . It is also causing growing alarm . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ongoing and routine attacks on health workers and violence are limiting the kind of help countries are sending . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;21091;TOOLONG ... title= " A member of the medical staff of the Ebola Treatment Unit ( ETU ) gets ready as she checks her Personal Protective Equipment ( PPE ) in front of the mirror . There have been reports that health teams responding to the outbreak have been attacked three or four times a week . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada , which is a member of WHO&amp;rsquo ; s Global Outbreak Alert and Response Network , a group that deals with threats of epidemic-prone diseases , responded to a request to send an emergency management specialist . That specialist will work at WHO&amp;rsquo ; s emergency operations centre in the neighbouring Republic of the Congo . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Unlike the previous West African crisis , the Public Health Agency of Canada has not , so far , sent epidemiologists , lab support or other experts directly to the outbreak region . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A PHAC spokesperson acknowledged Canada has a history of providing on-site support to WHO for Ebola . In addition to work developing the vaccine that has been deployed in DRC , the public health agency sent out epidemiologists , laboratory support and emergency management resources in 2014 and 2015 to support global efforts to respond to the West African Ebola epidemic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There have been no similar announcements this time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; When making decisions about how to support requests for assistance and subsequent deployments , &amp;rdquo ; said spokeswoman Maddison , &amp;ldquo ; PHAC considers where the greatest need exists and carefully considers the security of personnel . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;21157;TOOLONG ... title= " A health worker carries a four-day-old baby suspected of having Ebola , into a MSF ( Doctors Without Borders ) supported Ebola Treatment Centre ( ETC ) on November 4 , 2018 in Butembo , Democratic Republic of the Congo . The federal government has invested $1.5 million in rapid research aimed at improving the way the world responds to public health emergencies . It has also provided more than $3.3 million in emergency humanitarian assistance to WHO , the Red Cross , M&amp;eacute ; decins&amp;nbsp ; sans Fronti&amp;egrave ; res and other agencies that have health and support workers on the ground in DRC. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Steven Hoffman , scientific director of the Canadian Institutes of Health Research&amp;rsquo ; s ( CIHR ) Institute of Population and Public Health , noted that responding to the current outbreak has been challenging . &amp;ldquo ; It is happening in a part of a country which is experiencing significant conflict , which makes it much more difficult for organizations and governments to help out . &amp;rdquo ; It is also happening close to a large urban area , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Hoffman said the launch of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s rapid research fund for Ebola which supports four projects with African and Canadian researchers is an important accomplishment. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; From a Canadian perspective , this is a really good news story . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;21223;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; s International Development Research Centre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Four Canadian and African research teams were chosen to work on projects during the current Ebola surge &amp;mdash ; including studying how better access to routine health information could help control outbreaks and the role social , cultural and environmental factors play in containing them . &lt;/p&gt; I think the more people that can share their story , the more we can have conversations and the more that our society will be accepting . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Barrett , who fiercely defends his independence with cutting-edge technology , rigorous routine and the support of his wife &amp;mdash ; &amp;ldquo ; my rock&amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; chokes up as he recalls when that acceptance has been painfully missing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; One person I spoke with , the first words out of their mouth : &amp;lsquo ; You&amp;rsquo ; re still driving ? ' &amp;rdquo ; Barrett said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Take your time , &amp;rdquo ; said Meyers , 47 , putting a hand on his . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Keith makes really good decisions around where he drives and when he drives , &amp;rdquo ; she added . &amp;ldquo ; But I have people say to me , &amp;lsquo ; You still let him drive ? &amp;rsquo ; What do you mean , let him ? He&amp;rsquo ; s capable of making his own decisions . It&amp;rsquo ; s really a lack of understanding that people have that someone is very capable of contributing to their communities , to their lives , and to live . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;21681;TOOLONG ... title= " Keith Barrett , Robin Meyers and their dog Brooklyn .
