Steph Crosier/Postmedia " data-portal-copyright= " Steph Crosier " **28;26594;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1256427 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The LHIN called Ellen Toxopeus&amp;rsquo ; s son earlier this week to tell him that he and his brother Ron Toxopeaus would have to move their mother out of her unit by Friday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Luckily we found a place in Brockville , &amp;rdquo ; Ron said as he sat across from his mother , who had just finished lunch served by the Salvation Army . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ellen has been at Fairfield Manor East for about 20 years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I liked it here . It was a lovely place with the garden and the squirrels and the birds , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; But then this new owner was coo-coo. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ellen is on a waiting list to get into another local residence , closer to her two sons . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Kingston Fire &amp;amp ; Rescue continues to work closely with the LHIN and public health to ensure that health-care needs of residents and associated staff required at the retirement residence are on site until residents are relocated from Fairfield Manor , &amp;rdquo ; the news release said . &amp;ldquo ; It has been confirmed that food services at the privately owned facility remain in place . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Aug. 31 , the owner of Fairfield Manor Retirement Residence was charged for failing to comply with fire safety regulations applicable to buildings containing vulnerable occupants . On Tuesday , Armstrong told the Whig-Standard that , at the time , Qazi was facing seven charges under the Ontario Fire Code in relation to the west-end location and 16 for the east end . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In July , Fairfield Manor East lost its licence to operate from the Retirement Homes Regulatory Authority . At the time , Qazi said he&amp;rsquo ; d be appealing the decision . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Friday afternoon , a handwritten note was taped to the front door of Fairfield Manor East , next to the fire department&amp;rsquo ; s order to vacate . In it , the Heanes family thanked everyone for coming forward , praised the staff for their efforts and specifically thanked Payne . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You have a heart of gold , &amp;rdquo ; the note read . When the Whig-Standard left the Manor , Payne was still handing out mail to fellow residents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; scrosier@postmedia.com &lt;br&gt; Twitter : @StephattheWhig &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;This article originally appeared in &lt;a href= " https : **33;26624;TOOLONG ... Kingston Whig-Standard&lt;/a&gt;. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Steph CrosierSat , 08 Sep 2018 21:31:30 **45;26659;TOOLONG ... NewsSeniors'We are in crisis ' : Personal support workers are the backbone of home care in Ontario -- and there are n't enough of themhttp : **36;26706;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;26744;TOOLONG ... title= " Melanie Dea and her husband , Dr. Richard Martin . Richard has Huntington 's disease . " alt= " Melanie Dea and her husband , Dr. Richard Martin . Richard has Huntington 's disease . " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;26780;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1228514 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;26810;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Richard Martin slurs when he speaks . He has difficultly walking and swallowing food . He has short-term memory loss , and dementia is inevitable . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Martin has Huntington&amp;rsquo ; s disease , a neurodegenerative disorder . Until he was diagnosed in 2010 , Martin had a busy east-end medical practice . Today , he is cared for by his wife , Melanie Dea , in their Rockland home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dea has watched the disease attack her husband&amp;rsquo ; s mind and body . He needs constant attention and there&amp;rsquo ; s only so much Dea can provide . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And that&amp;rsquo ; s a problem . Because even though Martin is entitled to five hours and 15 minutes of home care every week , provided by a personal support worker , help is hard to find in rural Ontario and many other parts of the province . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Home care has been touted as a way to keep people out of hospitals and long-term care . It costs less than institutional care , it&amp;rsquo ; s better for patients and a life-saver for beleaguered caregivers like Dea . She relies on it , but her experience has been that her husband&amp;rsquo ; s entitlement does not translate into reality . Sometimes he will get the hours he is entitled to , but more often it will be only an hour or two , she says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There are so many PSWs who start and then they quit . Or they call in sick . I really need the break . I&amp;rsquo ; m burned out , &amp;rdquo ; says Dea. &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s a full-time job . I get three hours of respite a week . What do I do ? I go to the bank , to the grocery store . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Across Ontario , home care agencies are struggling to recruit and and retain personal support workers , known as PSWs . They get jobs right out of school , but many soon leave the field after they discover that the hours can be inconsistent , they are paid a dollar or two above minimum wage and they have to rush between appointments . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are in crisis across the province , &amp;rdquo ; says Miranda Ferrier , president of the 33,000-member Ontario Personal Support Workers Association . &amp;ldquo ; We have families calling us , desperate for a PSW. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;26846;TOOLONG ... title= " Melanie Dea and her husband , Dr. Richard Martin . " alt= " Melanie Dea and her husband , Dr. Richard Martin . " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;26882;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1228518 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; A survey of the associations&amp;rsquo ; members about four months ago drew more than 13,400 responses . It found that 79 per cent were unhappy with their jobs . Almost 39 per cent said their reason for dissatisfaction was staffing issues , while 26 per cent cited the pay scale , 24 per cent said it was an unsafe work environment and 11 per cent cited long or unpredictable hours . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; About a third of the respondents reported that they had already left the field . Almost two-thirds said they were burned out , 21 per cent said the job didn&amp;rsquo ; t pay enough and five per cent said they had been injured . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I hate to say it , but it&amp;rsquo ; s not an attractive job right now , &amp;rdquo ; says Ferrier . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; PSWs are even harder to recruit and retain in rural areas , where they have to travel long distances between assignments . &amp;ldquo ; The wear-and-tear on vehicles is immense . And who wants to travel for 45 minutes for a one-hour shift ? &amp;rdquo ; says Ferrier . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Chantale LeClerc , CEO of the Champlain Local Health Integration Network ( LHIN ) , which covers Ottawa and much of Eastern Ontario , says the situation is serious . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The LHIN is seeing the impact of a job market where supply doesn&amp;rsquo ; t meet demand . For much of 2015 and 2016 , it took less than 10 days on average for a client to receive a first home visit . That increased to an average of 77 days by June 2017 , before dropping as the result of an infusion of new funding from the province . But wait times for service is on its way up again . Clients waited an average of 28 days for service this May . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;26912;TOOLONG ... class= " size-full wp-image-1228517 " src= " https : **34;26947;TOOLONG ... alt= " " width= " 1000 " height= " 750 " srcset= " https : **31;26983;TOOLONG ... 1000w , https : **39;27016;TOOLONG ... 111w , https : **39;27057;TOOLONG ... 300w , https : **39;27098;TOOLONG ... 768w " sizes= " ( max-width : 1000px ) 100vw , 1000px " &gt;&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;27139;TOOLONG ... class= " size-full wp-image-1228516 " src= " https : **34;27174;TOOLONG ... alt= " " width= " 1000 " height= " 750 " srcset= " https : **31;27210;TOOLONG ... 1000w , https : **39;27243;TOOLONG ... 111w , https : **39;27284;TOOLONG ... 300w , https : **39;27325;TOOLONG ... 768w " sizes= " ( max-width : 1000px ) 100vw , 1000px " &gt;&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The LHIN&amp;rsquo ; s wait list peaked at just more than 3,000 clients in early 2017 . The list was whittled down last summer and into early fall last year because of the new funding , says LeClerc . But the wait lists started to grow again because the availability of workers could not keep pace with the demand . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s a complex issue , she says . PSW work can be challenging , and it&amp;rsquo ; s a mobile workforce that goes where working conditions or pay are better . There are issues around how people are deployed . Clients want workers in the morning and evening to coincide with getting up and going to bed , but workers don&amp;rsquo ; t want split shifts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have the funds available to serve patients , we have patients who need the services , but we don&amp;rsquo ; t have the human resources . In the past it was the funding . Now it&amp;rsquo ; s the human resources . It means people are waiting longer . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Home care workers are the bottom of the PSW hierarchy . While hospital PSWs can earn $26 an hour , those who work in home care earn only a little over $16 , says Ashley Brewster , a PSW in Prescott . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Home care PSWs might expect consistent work , but find their hours shrinking unexpectedly if a client is hospitalized or dies , she says . They pay high gas prices and get low mileage compensation . And they face potential violence from clients . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In home care , sometimes you don&amp;rsquo ; t know what you are walking into , &amp;rdquo ; Brewster says . &amp;ldquo ; In the end , we have a very hard job . It&amp;rsquo ; s hard on our bodies . It&amp;rsquo ; s hard on our minds . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **33;27366;TOOLONG : &amp;nbsp ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;27401;TOOLONG ... was win-win : ' The Perley and Algonquin College create joint PSW program&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Personal support workers say the time they have to spend with clients during home visits was scaled back following a much-hyped 2016 provincial pay raise . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sue VanderBent , CEO of Home Care Ontario , which represents providers of home care services across the province , began to notice the shortage of PSWs late last fall . She has seen increased anxiety and less satisfaction among PSWs as they see their days being chopped up into 15-minute segments . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While there have been frequent announcements of new money for home care &amp;mdash ; last October , the province announced that it would fund 1.5 million additional hours of personal support service &amp;mdash ; home care has remained about five per cent of the total health care budget for the past 20 years , VanderBent says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Even though we have seen investments , what&amp;rsquo ; s really happening is that it has kept pace with the growth of the whole system . We really haven&amp;rsquo ; t compensated for the demographic growth of the seniors population , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have created a situation where families get very tired and desperate . People are at the end of their ropes . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last October , Dea sought a few hours of respite care while she went to Montreal . Friends had agreed to stay with Martin for the first few hours , and were to be relieved by a pre-arranged respite worker . Dea checked the night before and was assured the worker would be there . But the worker never arrived , and Dea didn&amp;rsquo ; t know until she got a panicked call . Her vulnerable husband would be left alone in the house for two hours until she could get back from Montreal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dea was furious and wanted answers . &amp;ldquo ; All I got was &amp;lsquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m sorry&amp;rsquo ; , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If there is a shortage and a high demand , we have to see what we can do to get people into the field and stay in the field . If it&amp;rsquo ; s a problem with the pay grade , then that needs to be fixed . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;27436;TOOLONG ... title= " Melanie Dea and her husband , Dr. Richard Martin . " alt= " Melanie Dea and her husband , Dr. Richard Martin . " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;27472;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1228512 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ministry of Health and Long-Term Care is aware of the concerns and is working with LHINs , employers and other sector partners , says a ministry spokesman . &amp;ldquo ; Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s new government will be determining how to proceed now that it has been sworn in. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meanwhile , all of the players are working on solving the problem from different angles . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; PSWs are an unregulated profession , but they must be certified through an education program . Community colleges , private colleges and even school boards offer the program . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Julie Rivers , director of the PSW program at Loyalist College , says the college has just opened up to a second cohort of students . The program now has two groups of graduates every year , one that takes the traditional eight-month program and another that takes a compressed six-month program , which had 44 graduates this spring . Loyalist also offers programs in smaller rural communities such as Bancroft , Napanee and Barry&amp;rsquo ; s Bay . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They are scooped up before they even finish their program , &amp;rdquo ; says Rivers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Stephanie Lee , coordinator of the PSW program at the Perth campus of Algonquin College , says graduates prefer to work for a public-sector institution such as a hospital or a municipal nursing home because the pay is better , and there is more likelihood they will get consistent hours . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This year she had 19 graduates , and they all had a job offer . Many students work as a PSW and use it as a launching pad for further education as a paramedic , registered practical nurse or other profession . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the last provincial election , all three parties discussed the need for more money for home care . But none addressed the need to recruit and retain PSWs , says Lee . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s great . But how are we going to staff what we have now if we are not funding people to do this work ? You can&amp;rsquo ; t just hire anyone . You have to hire the right people and make sure they&amp;rsquo ; re learning the right skills . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Champlain LHIN is working with providers to look at different ways to schedule PSWs , and ways to assign them to a territory so they don&amp;rsquo ; t have to cover as much geographic area . And it&amp;rsquo ; s also looking at fast-tracking other health-care workers , such as paramedics and physiotherapy assistants into PSW roles without having to take the certification course , says LeClerc . The LHIN is also probing why some students graduate from PSW programs , then don&amp;rsquo ; t enter the field . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Part of the challenge is that there are potential causes and hypotheses , but little hard data . We know we need a range of solutions . There may be some we have not thought of , &amp;rdquo ; says LeClerc . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Home Care Ontario will be launching a campaign to attract not just young people new to the workforce , but also people contemplating a mid-life career shift . Offering classes in high school , so high school graduates will have some of the skills they need to do the job , is one option , says VanderBent. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; People want to do a good job . It has to be a fulfilling job , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &amp;ldquo ; We have created a system where it&amp;rsquo ; s not what it used to be . We have to bring that back . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some people who work in the field for 20 or 30 years are never dissatisfied , says Rivers . She believes that helping workers find the intrinsic value in what they do may be one of the keys to retaining workers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If you don&amp;rsquo ; t get some intrinsic value from the work , then you won&amp;rsquo ; t last long . Anyone who gets value out of the work will stay for a very long time . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2014 , a team of business researchers at McMaster University looked at &amp;ldquo ; task shifting , &amp;rdquo ; the delegating of certain tasks from regulated professionals to unregulated workers as a possible way to keep PSWs interested in their work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of the buzzwords in the business right now , for example , is &amp;ldquo ; virtual wards , &amp;rdquo ; a concept developed in the UK which uses the staffing of a hospital ward , without the actual building . PSWs would be the eyes and ears of a nurse , who would be connected remotely . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The McMaster researchers surveyed 46 home care workers about task shifting , including 20 PSWs . They found that half of the respondents thought task shifting would increase their intention to stay with the job . Learning new skills would increase their satisfaction with the job , and make them feel more accomplished , the workers reported . About a quarter said the opposite &amp;mdash ; task shifting would contribute to their intention to leave the home care sector because the new tasks would not be reflected in their pay , or the tasks would make them feel uncomfortable . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ferrier believes the PSW shortage can be eased by making it a regulated profession . &amp;ldquo ; I think it would change the entire dynamic of the profession . It would bring us the recognition we deserve . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Incentives such as better pay , more money for vehicle maintenance or free scrubs might also help , she says . &amp;ldquo ; There are all kinds of different things at play . But they will have to come up with something quick . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;By the numbers : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;83,000 : &lt;/strong&gt; Number of personal support workers practising in Ontario &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **35;27502;TOOLONG : Number who work in the home and community sector &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;180,000 : &lt;/strong&gt; Number of clients in Ontario who receive personal support services at home &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;13,427 : &lt;/strong&gt; Number of PSWs who responded to a survey conducted by the Ontario Personal Support Workers Association &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;33 per cent : &lt;/strong&gt; Proportion of these who said they quit in the past year &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;63.3 per cent : &lt;/strong&gt; Proportion who cited burnout as the reason &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;22 per cent : &lt;/strong&gt; Proportion of survey respondents who said they are considering leaving the field &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;52 per cent : &lt;/strong&gt; Proportion of these who cited burnout as the reason &lt;/p&gt; Joanne LauciusFri , 13 Jul 2018 12:20:17 **45;27539;TOOLONG ... " data-portal-copyright= " Jacqueline Larma " **28;27658;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1192769 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;27688;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; How many&amp;nbsp ; lives could be saved if cancer could&amp;nbsp ; be detected early and consistently ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Researchers are close to perfecting a new blood test that can screen for eight of the most common types of cancer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Watch the video to learn more . &lt;/p&gt; Staff ReporterFri , 11 May 2018 12:18:40 **45;27724;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; **35;27771;TOOLONG research centre a ' game changer ' for neuromuscular disease patientshttp : **32;27808;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;27842;TOOLONG ... title= " Dr. Jodi Warman , left , and Dr. Robin Parks in the newly opened Neuromuscular Clinical Research Centre at The Ottawa Hospital . " alt= " Dr. Jodi Warman , left , and Dr. Robin Parks in the newly opened Neuromuscular Clinical Research Centre at The Ottawa Hospital . " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;27878;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1195383 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa neurologist Dr. Jodi Warman Chardon recalls seeing patients with neuromuscular diseases &amp;mdash ; including muscular dystrophy , ALS and others &amp;mdash ; getting on the train to Montreal to take part in clinical trials not available in Ottawa . Some had to travel much farther . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The image has stayed with her . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; So many people are desperate to participate in clinical trials , &amp;rdquo ; she said . It made her realize more must be done to help make therapies and research available to Ottawa patients closer to home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ottawa Hospital&amp;rsquo ; s new neuromuscular research centre , which opened this week at the Civic campus , will enable patients to take part in clinical trials here in the capital , which will give them access to new treatments that are not otherwise available . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And it will do more than that , said Warman Chardon . The centre will create a hub in Ottawa for physicians and scientists to collaborate on new treatments . She calls it a &amp;ldquo ; game changer . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa has one of the highest concentrations of neuromuscular researchers in the world . The new centre will expand the work being done and bring patients and their physicians into the mix . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We want to be the Stanford of Canada , &amp;rdquo ; said Warman Chardon , who is also associate scientist at The Ottawa Hospital and assistant professor at the University of Ottawa . &amp;ldquo ; We have got a plan to become one of the largest neuromuscular research centres in North America , and the world . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;27908;TOOLONG ... title= " Dr. Jodi Warman. " alt= " Dr. Jodi Warman. " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;27944;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1195417 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among other things , the new centre will allow researchers to get access to blood samples from Ottawa patients to help with their research , said Dr. Robin Parks , a senior scientist at The Ottawa Hospital and professor at the University of Ottawa who helped establish the centre with Warman Chardon. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Patients and their families are also cheering the opening , which , according to hospital CEO Dr. Jack Kitts , gives Ottawa a glimpse of the kind of cutting-edge health care the new Civic campus will bring to the area when it opens . The new campus , to be built on the eastern edge of the Central Experimental Farm is in the planning stages . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s Teresa Buffone , who has family members with myotonic dystrophy , has been one of the forces pushing for the new centre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As a &amp;ldquo ; super advocate&amp;rdquo ; for people with myotonic dystrophy , Buffone has devoted recent years to raising awareness about , improving treatment for and expanding knowledge of the rare disease that can have a devastating impact on patients and their families . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She set up the country&amp;rsquo ; s first support group for patients with myotonic dystrophy as well as a patient registry and she led a fundraising drive for more research . That work was among efforts that paved the way for the centre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; People who have these neuromuscular diseases have multi-system difficulties and they just get worse over time . Having a centre where they can go and be followed on a routine basis means the specialists can affect medical interventions at an earlier stage rather than letting the disease progress to a critical stage . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She , too , said access to clinical trials in Ottawa will make a huge difference to the lives of patients with various neuromuscular diseases . Patients who wanted to participate in a trial involving myotonic dystrophy in the past have had to go to Rochester to do so , which was impossible for many . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She also said it is crucial to future breakthroughs that researchers are able to meet patients personally . She said she had been at conferences in the past in which researchers working on the disease had never met a myotonic dystrophy patient . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Researchers need to see the effectiveness of these diseases and how they manifest over time . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She said there are 10,000 people in Eastern Ontario with various neuromuscular disorders . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I am so happy that it finally came about , &amp;rdquo ; she said of the research centre . &lt;/p&gt; Elizabeth PayneWed , 02 May 2018 21:57:04 **45;27974;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal NewsMenSeniorsWomenAn active mind key to good health says Canada 's oldest blood donorhttp : **35;28021;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;28058;TOOLONG ... title= " Beatrice Janyk , 95 , smiles after donating blood at Canada Blood Services in Vancouver , B.C. , on Wednesday April 18 , 2018 .
